### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月通膨數據高於市場預期，CPI 月增 0.6%、年增 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增
0.3%、年增 2.6%。同月 PPI 月增 1.3%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最大增幅，年增
6.3%，顯示通膨壓力已逐步由能源價格擴散至更廣泛領域。數據公布後，市場一
度提高對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，但隨後多位官員釋出審慎訊號，使升息押注
降溫。聯準會理事鮑曼表示，決策前仍有時間觀察先前升息效果；舊金山聯準銀
行總裁戴莉則認為 10 月無立即升息之必要。而褐皮書顯示，美國就業市場維持
「低招聘、低裁員」格局，而高能源價格帶來的通膨壓力仍值得關注。
⚫ 中國 9 月通膨持續回升，CPI 年增 0.9%，創近三個月高點；PPI 年增 5.0%，創
逾四年最大漲幅，顯示通縮壓力進一步減輕。主要受能源與原物料價格上漲帶
動，且 9 月進出口表現優於預期，AI 產業鏈需求與貿易環境改善提供支撐；不

過，信貸增長仍仰賴政府發債與季底效應，內需與民間融資動能仍偏弱。
⚫ 日本銀行審議委員小枝淳子表示，不宜對升息過於保守，並認為政策利率仍有進
一步上調空間。總裁植田和男則強調維持 2%通膨目標的重要性。整體而言，日
本央行持續釋出偏鷹派訊號，有助支撐日圓表現。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委溫施表示，在能源價格波動下，為了控制通膨仍不排除進一步升息
可能，總裁拉加德則認為法國債務問題應透過財政政策解決，歐洲央行官員普遍
認為能源價格的潛在波動依然是推升通膨並使其居高不下的主要風險來源。。
⚫ 澳洲央行會議紀錄指出，能源價格上漲與需求韌性可能使通膨更具黏性，保留
進一步升息空間。然而 9 月就業數據意外疲弱，市場大幅下調升息預期，目前
預估 11 月維持利率不變的機率接近 90%。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Oct/19/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.890，低點在 32.520，以 32.338 作收，較前一周收盤價
貶值 4 角 6.3 分。
上週台幣走貶，周初，外資延續前周大幅匯出的走勢，雖逢高吸引出口商拋匯，
仍不敵外資匯出力道令台幣走貶；週二台股重挫近千點，外資大幅賣超台股 1653 億
並匯出，台幣接連跌破 32.000、32.100 及 32.200，總成交量 71.3 億，創歷史新高；
周三台股收漲，外資雙向進出，台幣收斂部分跌幅；週四，受美國通膨高於預期影
響，早盤台幣跳空開低，台股跌逾 2%，外資大幅賣超 3025 億，台幣跌破 31.400 關
卡，總成交量 91.2 億，再創歷史新高；週五外資持續匯出，台幣貶至周內低點 32.520，
終場在央行調節之下收在 32.338。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 CPI、PPI 雙雙高於預期，通

膨壓力仍在，儘管聯準會官員陸續發言表示不急於升息，淡化市場對於聯準會十月
升息預期，惟年底前聯準會仍有升息一碼空間，利率持穩於高位將給予國際美元支
撐；就台灣市場來看，市場對 AI 產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，若
賣壓延續，將壓抑市場風險偏好，增加外資調整台股部位及匯出的壓力，令台幣面
臨持續貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯將抑制台幣貶值速度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣
走勢偏弱。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.100-32.700

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/19/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。上週人民幣中間價連續走升，週三中間價升至 6.7249 創
三年八個月新高，惟市場靜待美國公布通膨數據，交投謹慎，人民幣僅小幅走升；
週三晚間，美國公布 9 月 CPI 年增率 3.7%、核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，雙雙高於預期，
帶動美元指數升至年內高點 102.945、人民幣貶至周內低點 6.7005；然多位聯準會官
員發言表示聯準會不急於升息，令市場對於聯準會十月升息預期略有降溫，美元指
數自高點回落，激勵人民幣由貶轉升；臨近周末，聯準會主席華許於 IMF 會議發言
並未指引十月聯準會政策利率走向，再度淡化市場對於聯準會十月升息預期，國際
美元走勢偏弱，人民幣走升觸及年內高點 6.6825，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6850，週
線升值 91 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6870，週線升值 60 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。就國際市場來看，美國仍面臨高通膨、年底可能將
再次升息，利差因素支撐美元指數於高檔震盪，限縮人民幣升值空間；就中國國內
市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實質拋匯需求將支撐人
民幣表現；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，匯價維持在五日均線之下交
投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以 USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.6900 整數關卡，顯示 USD/CNH
空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪偏升。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6500-6.7000

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 先上後下。週初，受油價攀升推升美國通膨與利率預期帶動，美
元指數溫和走升創 6 月底以來新高，推升 USD/JPY 突破 158.50；週間，美國公布 9
月 CPI 高於預期，帶動美元指數升至 2025 年 5 月以來高點，也使 USD/JPY 最高來
到 158.94，然逢高獲利了結賣壓出籠使匯價回落；臨近週末，USD/JPY 一度下探至
157.53，隨後逢低買盤進場收復部分跌幅，終場收在 157.99，週線下跌 13 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受美伊戰事推升汽柴油飆漲
與服務成本擴散影響，美國 9 月 CPI 及 PPI 月增漲幅均超出市場預期，加上初請失
業金人數連五週低於 20 萬人顯示勞動市場穩定，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月再次升
息，美日實質利差維持高檔，持續為 USD/JPY 提供下檔支撐。二、被視為鴿派的日
本經濟財政大臣城內實明確表態，貨幣政策應交由日本央行自行判斷，並宣告日本
已擺脫長期通縮、轉型為增長導向經濟體。此番言論降低政治干預日銀進一步升息
的疑慮，在貨幣政策正常化的腳步下，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。三、就技
術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價目前交投於月線及年線之間，各天期均線高度
糾結，多空雙方於 158 關卡附近拉鋸，在有效突破季線及年線反壓或跌破月線支撐
前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：157.00-159.00 
前週交易區間 
157.53 158.94

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
歐元兌美元匯率走勢  
         Oct/19/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，持續受法國財政赤字與歐債利差擔憂拖累，
EUR/USD 震盪走弱；週間，因美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期，市場重新評估聯準會年底前
升息腳步，導致 EUR/USD 跳水最低來到 1.1112；臨近週末，儘管美國 PPI 與零售
銷售數據偏熱，市場優先反映美債殖利率回落與風險資產重新定價，美元多頭獲利
了結帶動 EUR/USD 谷底反彈，最高來到 1.1269，終場收在 1.1239，週線上漲 42 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、法國央行月度展望報
告指出，法國第三季 GDP 預估僅微幅成長 0.1%，印證近期高能源的成本已實質拖
累歐元區核心成員國增長。同時，歐洲央行總裁拉加德明確拒絕出手干預法債，要
求法國透過自身財政政策化解赤字困境；在明年法國大選前的財政隱憂與基本面疲
弱下，預計將令歐元上檔持續承壓。二、儘管歐洲央行管委溫施與總裁拉加德保留
12 月升息預期，加上德國基建與軍費支出為歐元提供部分下檔保護，但市場穩固預
期聯準會年底前將再升息一次。在美國經濟韌性與高利差優勢主導下，預計將限縮
歐元反彈空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 自低點反彈並站回五日線
之上，然而中長期均線下彎仍維持空頭排列，RSI 指標亦處於 50 中軸下方，顯示整
體空方趨勢尚未扭轉，反彈至月線附近恐面臨較大賣壓。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1150-1.1300 
前週交易區間 
1.1112 1.1269

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115. 10.12-115.10.16 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.020~2.025% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.88%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週央行連續減發定存單使市場資金寬鬆，雖外資大舉匯
出，資金供給仍充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.58%，低於市場
預期區間 1.60%-1.62%，較上期標售結果下跌 5.8 個基本點。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 5 年期指標券 A15108 週二搓合成交在 1.88%，後隨美債利率滑落，加上本週
2 年期公債標債結果提振市場持券信心，帶動 10 年期指標券 A15109 週四向
下成交在 2.02%~2.025%。 
在銀行去化短期資金及補充 LCR 之需求下，週三 2 年期公債得標利率為 1.65%，
落在市場預估區間下緣，投標倍數回升至 2.86 倍，銀行業得標比重上升至 88%。
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率大幅回檔，促使台債交投偏空氛圍轉趨和
緩，惟在央行維持緊縮貨幣政策下，買盤積極追價的意願不強，預期短線台債
殖利率呈區間格局的可能性較高，關注下週 10 年券標售結果，預估 5 年券區
間為 1.85%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.98%~2.03%。

四、前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元
10月末，本外币存款余额358.81万亿元，同比增长7.8%。月末人民币存款余额350.65万亿元，同比增长7.7%。前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元。其中，住户存款增加8.47万亿元，非金融企业存款增加1.91万亿元，财政性存款增加3.03万亿元，非银行业金融机构存款
增加5.99万亿元。10月末，外币存款余额1.22万亿美元，同比增长16.9%。前十个月外币存款增加1553亿美元。
五、前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元
10月末，本外币贷款余额287.25万亿元，同比增长4.6%。月末人民币贷款余额283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%。前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元。分部门看，住户贷款减少1.46万亿元，其中，短期贷款减少1.41万亿元，中长期贷款减少501亿元；企（事）业单位贷款
增加12.39万亿元，其中，短期贷款增加4.65万亿元，中长期贷款增加6.69万亿元，票据融资增加9283亿元；非银行业金融机构贷款减少2395亿元。10月末，外币贷款余额5831亿美元，同比增长5.2%。前十个月外币贷款增加381亿美元。
六、10月份银行间人民币市场同业拆借月加权平均利率为1.3%，质押式债券回购月加权平均利率为1.33%
10月份银行间人民币市场以拆借、现券和回购方式合计成交167.98万亿元，日均成交9.33万亿元，日均成交同比增长1.9%。其中，同业拆借日均成交同比下降10.8%，现券日均成交同比增长4.7%，质押式回购日均成交同比增长1.3%。10月份同业拆借加权平均利率为1.3%，
比上月低0.1个百分点，比上年同期低0.09个百分点；质押式回购加权平均利率为1.33%，比上月低0.07个百分点，比上年同期低0.07个百分点。
七、10月份经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元
10月份，经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，其中货物贸易、服务贸易及其他经常项目分别为1.43万亿元、0.47万亿元；直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元，其中对外直接投资、外商直接投资分别为0.21万亿元、0.35万亿元。

2026年10月金融数据

2026年11月13日，人民银行发布2026年1-10月金融统计数据报告。数据显示，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长
4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。
一、社会融资规模存量同比增长7.2%
初步统计，2026年10月末社会融资规模存量为469.16万亿元，同比增长7.2%。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款余额279.31万亿元，同比增长4.6%；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币余额1.26万亿元，同比增长9%；委托贷款余额11.53万亿元，同比增长1.7%；信托
贷款余额4.49万亿元，同比下降0.6%；未贴现的银行承兑汇票余额2.05万亿元，同比下降4.4%；企业债券余额36.92万亿元，同比增长9.6%；政府债券余额106.41万亿元，同比增长14.4%；非金融企业境内股票余额12.77万亿元，同比增长5.5%。
二、前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元
初步统计，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款增加10.91万亿元，同比少增3.61万亿元；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币增加2546亿元，同比多增3692亿元；委托贷款增加2084亿
元，同比多增1002亿元；信托贷款减少1798亿元，同比多减3958亿元；未贴现的银行承兑汇票减少939亿元，同比多减1055亿元；企业债券净融资3.1万亿元，同比多1.27万亿元；政府债券净融资11.49万亿元，同比少4592亿元；非金融企业境内股票融资5705亿元，同比多
1842亿元。

Document 136: 261113_daiwa_Gigabyte

### Page 1

Summary: Gigabyte released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 13 November, with 
net income of TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beating expectations, while revenue of 
TWD154.7bn (+7% QoQ, +94% YoY) was largely in line despite robust server demand. 
Gross margin expanded 4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% (vs. our 9.6% estimate). Management cited 
factors such as higher value-added products and better cost control, though we believe 
VGA card price hikes were the key driver. Despite strong gross margin expansion and 
operating leverage, higher profit-sharing bonuses limited OPM expansion to 2.7pp QoQ to 
8.3%, still above our/consensus estimates of 5.8%/5.9%. We expect Gigabyte to benefit 
from solid AI server demand from neoclouds as they increase power budgets and capex, 
with neoclouds and enterprises becoming key Nvidia server buyers from Vera Rubin in 
2027. Nebius reaffirmed its 5GW contracted-power and 0.8-1GW connected-power end-
2026 targets, with >1GW annual deployments from 2027; CoreWeave raised its 2026 
capex guidance to USD37-39bn, with USD132.5bn in backlog. As neoclouds serve 
sovereign and enterprise customers lacking in-house hardware capabilities, we expect 
TAM growth for server brands including Dell, Gigabyte, Lenovo and Asustek. We reaffirm 
our AI server revenue forecasts of TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E. We 
expect the AI server mix to dilute the GM to 8.9% in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E. We 
reiterate our Buy (1) rating with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on 15x the 1-year-forward 
diluted EPS; key downside risk is Nvidia’s business model change.

What’s the impact: 
Below are our read-throughs from Gigabyte’s 3Q26 results (released on 13 November).

• 3Q26 results first-take: profitability beat expectations. Gigabyte’s net income of 
TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beat our/Bloomberg consensus estimates by 32%/29%, 
respectively. Revenue (TWD154.7bn; up 7% QoQ and 94% YoY) was largely in-line with 
estimates, despite robust server demand. Nevertheless, the gross margin expanded by 
4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% in 3Q26 (exceeding our estimate of 9.6%). Management attributed 
the strength to an improved product mix, higher value-added products, strategic 
procurement, and better cost control, but we believe price hikes in VGA cards to be the 
key factor. Despite strong gross margin expansion and operating leverage, higher profit-
sharing bonus expenses caused the operating margin to only rise by 2.7pp QoQ to 8.3% 
(still beating our/street’s estimates of 5.8%/5.9%).

• Business outlook. We believe Gigabyte will ride on consistently solid AI server demand 
from the neoclouds, as key vendors continue to raise their power budget and capex. 
This aligns with our view that the neoclouds and enterprise will gradually become key

buyers of Nvidia AI servers starting from the Vera Rubin generation in 2027. Nebius 
(NBIS US, NR) reaffirmed its end-2026 contracted-power target of 5GW (up from >4GW 
currently contracted) and connected-power target of 0.8-1GW, with plans to deploy 
>1GW annually starting in 2027. Meanwhile, Coreweave (CRWV US, NR) raised its 
2026 capex guidance to USD37-39bn (from USD35-39bn) with an order backlog of 
USD132.5bn (vs. USD3.7bn revenue in 3Q26). We believe the neoclouds will become a 
key computing source for sovereign, enterprise and other corporate customers that 
cannot design and maintain the hardware themselves. As such, we expect the TAM for 
server brands like Dell (DELL US, USD371.17, Outperform [2], covered by Louis 
Miscioscia), Gigabyte, Lenovo (992 HK, NR) and Asustek (2357 TT, NR) to rise.

While management expects continued revenue growth and improving operating 
leverage in 2027, we reaffirm our AI server revenue assumptions for Gigabyte of 
TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E (vs. TWD180bn in 2025). We also 
believe the rising contribution from AI servers will be structurally margin-dilutive, despite 
potential benefits from higher value-added solutions, strategic procurement, and 
economies of scale. We therefore forecast gross margin to gradually normalise to 8.9% 
in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E (vs. 14.0% in 3Q26).

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Gigabyte, with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on a target PER 
of 15x, applied to our 1-year-forward diluted EPS. Key downside risk: earnings impact from 
Nvidia’s business model change.

For Pegatron, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$514bn (+32% QoQ, +61% YoY), as 
we expect to see more contribution from AI server going into 4Q26. We expect GM to 
slightly decrease to 4.2% (-0.3ppt QoQ and +0.1%ppt YoY). We continue to see 4Q26 
revenue with QoQ growth on a continuing ramp-up in AI server shipments as well as the 
continued peak season for its communication business. 
For Compal, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$282bn (+16% QoQ, +48% YoY) with 
increasing contribution from AI server  as the company continue to expand from 
motherboard (L6) to system level (L10/L11). We expect GM to slightly increase to 4.6% 
(+0.2ppt QoQ and -1.2ppt YoY) as the YoY contraction is due to shift in product mix (we 
expect PC business to account for 60% in 4Q26 vs. 71% in 4Q25).  
For Asus, we expect 4Q26 revenue to reach NT$283bn (+2% QoQ, +40% YoY), with solid 
growth from its server business, offsetting the weakness from its PC business, although 
we continue to expect a more premium PC product mix. We expect GM to decrease to 
14.8% (-1.6ppt QoQ and -1.1ppt YoY) due to the change in product mix.

### Page 3

Earnings revisions 
Quanta 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 3% to factor in 3Q26 results (we revise 
up our 2026E revenue estimates by 2%). We revise down 2027/28E earnings estimates 
by 7%/1% to reflect a product mix with higher contribution from the server business (we 
revise up our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 1/2%). We now expect the GM to contract 
going forward due to the higher server business contribution, thus revise down GPM by 
0.3/0.2/0.1ppt in 2026/27/28E.

Pegatron 
We revise down our 2026 earnings estimates by 20% to factor in 3Q26 results (we 
maintain our 2026E revenue estimates). We revise down 2027 earnings estimates by 5% 
to reflect a higher % for minority interest while revise up 2028 earnings estimates by 3% 
with improved GM (we revise down our 2027/28E revenue estimates by 9/6% to reflect 
a more conservative assumption for its communication business). We now expect the 
GM to remain stable going forward, thus revise up GPM by 0.4/0.7/0.5ppt in 
2026/27/28E.

Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Quanta 
Quanta is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. We are Neutral rated on Quanta. We expect the 
company’s earnings to grow in 2026-28E, driven by 1) growing AI server demand from 
Quanta’s CSP customers, and 2) general server demand recovery. However, we see P/E 
valuation as fair at the current level, given the positives appear to have been mostly 
factored in the share price and the current upside to our target price is relatively lower 
compared to its peers under coverage. We would consider becoming more positive if the 
AI server demand from its CSP clients turn out to be above our expectations.

### Page 7

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$280 is based on a 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E.  
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers. 
Pegatron is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of 
smartphones, PCs, consumer electronics, and servers, etc. While we like Pegatron’s 
product line diversification into servers, AI servers, and automotive electronics, we are 
Sell rated as we see revenue contribution from AI server remaining small in near-term, 
and our 2027E target P/E is near the company’s current trading P/E. We expect 
smartphone business will remain the major revenue contributor from 2026E to 2028E, 
and we see the AI server related revenue contribution remains 4% in 2026E (vs. peers 
14%~87%). Catalysts for further upside will be its faster-than-expected progress in AI 
servers. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Pegatron 
We are Sell rated on Pegatron with a 12-month target price of NT$71. Our TP is based 
on a 9.7x 2027E P/E, which is the company’s 2018-23 average P/E. 
Key upside risks: 1) stronger-than-expected smartphone market recovery, 2) 
faster-than-expected AI server ramp-up, and 3) stronger-than-expected demand on 
consumer electronics.

Investment thesis- Compal 
Compal is an ODM company focusing on the design and manufacturing of PCs, tablets, 
consumer electronics, servers, etc. While we like Compal’s solid position in the PC ODM 
market and its expansion into AI servers, we are Neutral rated as we see the contribution 
from AI servers as limited in the near-term given its limited exposure to cloud server 
customers, who are the early adopters of AI servers. Meanwhile, we believe valuation 
already reflects near-term positives. We model Compal earnings growth in 2026-26E to 
be mainly driven by commercial PC demand recovery and server business ramping up. 
We see P/E valuation as fair at current levels compared to peers given its mild earnings 
growth in 2026-28E.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Compal 
We are Neutral-rated on Compal with a 12-month target price of NT$45. Our target 
price is based on 14.7x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger/weaker-than-expected demand for tablets.

### Page 8

Investment thesis- Asus 
Asus is a multinational computer hardware and electronics company based in Taiwan 
and is the top motherboard and gaming brand globally and also a top-three consumer 
notebook vendor, per the company. We are Neutral rated on Asus considering (1) 
challenging PC market under end of Win10 replacement cycle, rising memory costs, and 
consumption pull in in 1H25 under China government subsidies and tariff concerns, (2) 
ASUS brand AI servers remain small in shipment. The stock currently trades at 14x 2027E 
PE, close to our target PE multiple (14.8x), which we think is fair.  
We would turn more positive on ASUS if we see (1) its PC revenue growth stronger than 
expected which could be driven by faster than expected product mix upgrade toward AI 
PC or gaming PC, or (2) faster than expected AI servers shipment ramp up, which could 
be driven by penetration in leading enterprises or CSPs (cloud services providers).

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Asus 
We are Neutral-rated on ASUS with a 12-month target price of NT$1,000. Our target 
price is based on 14.8x 2027E P/E, which is +0.5 STDV above the company’s 2018-23 
average P/E 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger / weaker-than-expected PC market 
growth; 2) faster / slower-than-expected AI and gaming PC growth; and 3) faster / 
slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this

3Q26 EPS 低於市場預期 14%，預期 4Q26 毛利率小幅回升 
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，其中伺服器營收年增
55%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，原物料成本維持高檔，
加上新平台導入慢於預期，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%。薪資費用高
於預期以及業外淨收益亦較上季下降，EPS 為 17.06 元，低於本中心/市場
預期 11.0%/14.4%。考量缺料延續及新平台挹注有限，下修 4Q26 營收
5.8%至 103.89 億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%，毛利率估季增 0.3 個百分點
至 45.1%，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元。 
QD 擴產及伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30% 
QD 產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增，預估 QD 占總營收比重於
2026 年 12 月達 3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。AMD Venice 預計自 1Q27
末加速放量，2027 年平均採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27
末至 2Q27 初導入，2H27 採用率估達 10–15%。新平台有助提升 CPU 
Socket 單價及 PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率，預估 2027 年伺服器營收年增 50%，
帶動整體營收年增 30%。 
維持買進及目標價 2,200 元 
伺服器缺料及 PC 業務疲弱使營收低於前估，原物料成本居高、新平台滲透
慢於預期，加上低於公司平均毛利率的 QD 占比提高及薪資費用增加，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元。評價方面，本中心仍看
好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益
率由 24.5%回升至 28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 
2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比推得目標價 2,200 元。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS 為 17.06 元，低於市場預期 14%，主因薪資費用提升以及
原物料成本居高、新平台導入進度不如預期，毛利率季減至 44.8%。 
⧫  
記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，下修 4Q26 EPS 
12.6%至 18.79 元，預期毛利率小幅回升。 
⧫  
QD 放量與 AMD/Intel 新平台導入帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%、EPS
年增 42%至 106.29 元，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元。

營運分析
3Q26 毛利率低於預期，薪資費用提升，EPS 低於市場預期 14%
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，低於本中心/市場預期 1.7%/2.5%，其中伺服
器營收年增 55%，占總營收 53.1%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續限制客戶出貨，CPU Socket 拉貨
低於預期。原物料成本維持高檔，伺服器新平台導入慢於預期，加上部分桌機客戶因缺料改採支援
DDR4 或 DDR3 的舊平台，使低單價、低毛利產品占比偏高，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%，
低於本中心/市場預期 0.7/0.9 個百分點。中國廠區自 2Q26 起調薪，使費用率年增約 0.7 個百分點。
受惠於營收規模擴大，費用率仍季減 1.6 個百分點至 21.9%，營益率升至 22.9%，營業利益季增
8.6%至 22.84 億元。業外淨收益由上季 4.95 億元降至 0.95 億元，歸屬母公司淨利季減 3.3%至

19.19 億元。EPS 為 17.06 元，季減 3.5%/年減 17.6%，低於本中心/市場預期 11.0%/14.4%。

新平台導入速度不如預期，下修 4Q26 EPS 13%至 18.79 元
嘉澤 10 月營收 32.0 億元，月減 5%/年增 12%，達本中心 4Q26 營收原估的 29%。公司預期 4Q26
營收呈低個位數季增，零組件缺料仍是主要出貨限制，因此本中心下修 4Q26 營收 5.8%至 103.89
億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%。SOCAMM 等新品出貨增加有助毛利率回升，惟 AMD Venice 年底
採用率僅估 3–5%，Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，高針腳數 CPU Socket

對 4Q26 產品組合的挹注低於前估，加上原物料成本維持高檔，以及毛利率低於公司平均的 QD 出
貨增加，本中心下修 4Q26 毛利率 1.4 個百分點至 45.1%，季增 0.3 個百分點。預估營業利益季增
10.2%至 25.17 億元，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元，季增約 10%/年減 16.5%。

QD 擴產與伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%
本中心預估嘉澤 2027 年營收年增 30%，主要成長動能來自 QD 放量與通用伺服器平台換代。公司
預估 2027 年伺服器整機出貨量年增 10–15%，新平台導入也將推升 CPU Socket 單價，並帶動
PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率提升。AMD Venice 目前採用率不到 3%，預計自 1Q27 末加速放量，2027
年全年採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，2H27 採用率估達

10–15%，營收貢獻主要落在下半年。隨 PCIe 連接器與線材市占率提升，本中心預估 2026/2027
年伺服器營收分別年增 56%/50%。嘉澤另預計於 2H27 開始出貨 Arm CPU 平台的客製化 Socket。
QD 目前產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增。預估 QD 占總營收比重於 2026 年 12 月達
3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。QD 放量將稀釋整體毛利率，但新增營業費用相對有限，規模擴大
後仍可挹注營業利益及 EPS。隨伺服器新平台採用率提高及 SOCAMM 出貨增加，本中心預估
2027 年毛利率由 46.2%回升至 48.5%。NPO Socket 預計於 2027 年以總頻寬 3.2Tbps 的產品小量
出貨，占營收不到 2%，2028 年隨單價較高的 6.4Tbps 產品導入，營收貢獻將更為明顯。
非伺服器業務方面，公司預估 2027 年 PC 市場出貨量再減約 10%。本中心估嘉澤 2026/2027 年筆
電營收分別年減 17%/4%，桌機營收年減 30%/年增 1%;策略客戶營收年增 3%/2%，車用年增

27%/15%，工業及醫療年增 22%/20%。嘉基受惠於高速線材出貨成長，營收預估年增 12%/40%。

產業概況  
2026 年 PC 出貨恐衰退近雙位數 
記憶體價格大漲，影響 PC 出貨。受到上游記憶體成本大幅上揚，PC 廠商即使轉嫁或降低規格，
均將影響消費者購買意願，因此預估 2026 年 PC(NB+DT)出貨衰退 9.6%，NB 出貨亦年減 9.7%。
2027 年 PC 及 NB 出貨量才有機會回升低個位數。 
上半年消費者提前拉貨，下半年出貨待觀察。1H26 市場預期記憶體及儲存裝置未來大漲，因此客
戶有提前拉貨現象，IDC 統計全球 PC 出貨 679 萬台，季減 16%，但年增 3%，2Q26 起 PC 步入
衰退 5%，下半年更衰退至 15~20%。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第6 頁，共 16頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026/2027 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 95%/46%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic)、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國主要 CSP 資本支出將年增 95%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA 的天下，AWS、Google、Meta 
ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將採購
TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，2026/2027 年通用型伺服器預估出貨量將年增
10%/9%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP
等業者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中
將消耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第7 頁，共 16頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
15%及 31%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者，2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下，CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本，ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

### Page 8

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第8 頁，共 16頁
獲利調整與股票評價
下修 2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元
伺 服 器 供 應 鏈 缺 料 持 續 壓 抑 CPU Socket 拉 貨 ， 筆 電 及 桌 機 營 收 亦 低 於 前 估 ， 本 中 心 下 修
2026/2027 年營收 2.0%/2.6%至 392.43/510.58 億元，分別年增 16.2%/30.1%。原物料成本維持高
檔，AMD 及 Intel 新平台採用率提升慢於預期，加上 QD 營收占比提高，使毛利率改善幅度低於前
估。本中心下修 2026/2027 年毛利率 0.5/0.6 個百分點至 46.2%/48.5%。費用方面，中國廠區調薪
推升固定薪資支出，公司預估 2027 年單季營業費用約 22–25 億元。考量薪資費用高於前估，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元，分別年增 6.8%/41.9%。

評價方面，本中心仍看好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益率由 24.5%回升至
28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比
推得目標價 2,200 元。

公司簡介  
嘉澤成立於 1986 年，專門生產及設計 PCIe、DDR、SATA 等連接器及 USB 連接埠，公司擁有完整的生產流程，包含設計、
加工、成型、沖壓、電鍍及自動化組裝，可提供客製化需求及標準型產品。公司為全球前三大 Server Socket (CPU 插槽)供
應商，市佔率約為 30-35%，同業為鴻海集團(鴻騰精密)及安費諾等。該產業的進入門檻高，多數產品皆已申請專利，且公
司實驗室已通過 ISO 認證，可縮短測試時間，並降低測試費用。嘉澤產品為電腦、伺服器、消費性電子產品之各式連接器
及零組件，旗下有四大事業體，分別是 PC/NB、伺服器、子公司嘉基及其他業務(包括 Type-C 連接器)。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 嘉澤整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於中間偏前的
位置，在電子零組件行業中的公司中排名略領先於同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 嘉澤的整體曝險屬於中等水準，優於電子零組件行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的 ESG 議
題包含公司治理、商業道德與資源使用等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：嘉澤在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司遵循適當的 ESG 揭露，
表明對投資者和公眾的強烈責任感。其 ESG 相關問題由董事會或經營團隊負責，建議將這些整合到核心業務戰略中。

證券發行機構之看法；(2) 研究部分析師於本研究報告中所提出之特定投資建議或觀點，與其過去、現在、未來薪酬的任何部份皆無直接或間接關聯。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성바이오로직스 (207940)

Company Brief 
제약/바이오 
Analyst 김준영 
junyoung.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 김현비 
hyunbee.kim@meritz.co.kr 외형 성장 지속, 수익성 개선은 시간이 필요

3Q26 Review  
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(YoY +18.5%, QoQ +12.8%), 영업이익 
6,401억원(YoY +1.0%, QoQ +9.2%)으로 컨센서스를 각각 9.8%, 4.7% 상회했다. 
기존 1 ~ 4공장 풀가동 및 미국 Rockville 공장 매출 약 1,000억원과 5공장 PPQ 
물량 인식이 더해지며 외형 성장을 견인했다. 반면 신규 생산시설의 초기 비용 및 제
품 믹스 영향으로 OPM은 42.9%를 기록하며 전년 동기 대비 7.5%p 하락했다. 동
사는 연간 매출 성장률 가이던스를 기존 +15~20%에서 +20%로 상향했다. 당사는 
4분기 Rockville 공장 매출 기여와 기존 공장의 높은 가동률을 감안하여 연간 매출
액 5조 6,135억원(YoY +23.2%)을 예상한다.

신규 생산거점의 외형 확대, 이익 기여는 좀 더 긴 호흡으로 
5공장은 2Q26부터 PPQ 생산을 본격화했으나 상업 생산에 따른 실질적인 이익 기
여는 2027년 하반기에 확대될 전망이다. Rockville 공장은 3Q와 4Q 각각 약 
1,000억원의 매출이 예상되며 2027년 분기별 매출도 유사한 수준을 유지할 것으로 
전망한다. 다만 영업이익 기여까지는 시간이 더 소요될 것으로 예상되어 2027년 이
익 성장은 미국 공장보다 5공장 Ramp-up이 주도할 전망이다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원 유지 
삼성바이오로직스에 대해 투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 200만원을 유지한다. 고객사 수주 
문의가 회복되는 가운데 대형 계약 협의가 진행 중이며 6공장 역시 연말 또는 내년 
초 착공을 목표로 하고 있다. 다만 신규 수주가 실제 계약으로 이어지는 시점과 5공
장의 상업 생산 전환 속도가 중기 실적의 핵심 변수로 판단한다. 추가 대형 수주 확
보 시 5공장 이후의 성장 가시성도 높아질 것으로 예상한다. 
PolyPeptide 인수는 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업을 확장한다는 점에서 긍정적이다. 인
수 완료 이후 다음 분기부터 연결 매출 인식이 가능하나 유의미한 영업이익 기여까지
는 최소 2년 이상 소요될 전망이다. PolyPeptide 인수는 현재 추정치에서 반영하지 
않았으며 향후 인수 완료 시 연결 OPM 희석 가능성을 감안할 필요가 있다. 한편 최
근 법원 항고심에서 배양부터 정제까지의 생산공정이 쟁의행위 기간에도 정상 수행되
어야 할 보전작업으로 인정되면서 파업에 따른 생산 차질 우려는 완화될 것으로 판단
한다. 노사 협상은 여전히 진행 중이나 생산 안정성에 대한 고객사 우려가 낮아질 경
우 신규 수주에도 긍정적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 22

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 2,000,000원 
현재주가 (10.21) 1,417,000원 
상승여력 41.1%

표5  타법인 주식 양수결정: PolyPeptide Group AG 인수 개요 
항목 내용

Document 332: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-21).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-21
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

M: +64 21 710 852

► 近一週美股、日股及新興市場表現遜於港股
及歐股，因市場擔憂民主黨全面掌控國會恐
影響AI基礎建設擴張進程，令股市承壓，即
便總統川普表態接受共和黨敗選結果並願尋
求民 主 黨 的跨 黨 合 作，仍 無 法緩 解 市 場擔
憂，美股主要指數普遍收跌；歐股方面，面
對全球科技股評價修正，部分資金轉入歐洲
市場尋求避險，使歐股近期呈現跌深反彈；

亞股方面，日股因整體AI供應鏈與美國高度
連結而同步下跌，港股則是受惠騰訊發布新
AI模型帶動相關版塊大漲。
► 市場擔憂美股科技巨頭財報不如預期、AI基
礎建設受限及資本開支見頂、負自由現金流
量等因素，導致市場資金從半導體、資訊科
技與 通 訊 服務 股 大 幅流 出 ，並轉 進 必 需消
費、醫療保健、公用事業等防禦性產業。

► 美國期中選舉結果揭曉，民主黨掌握參、眾
兩院多數席次，將壓縮財政擴張幅度，政策
方向著重解決人民生活成本上升問題、加速
解決美 伊戰 爭問 題 ，令能 源價格 從高 點回
落，在預期通膨降溫、利率回落及風險情緒
推升下，令 資金 流 入債市 推升全 球債 券價
格 ， 包 含 美 國2 、10 年 期 公 債 、 全 球 投 等
債、非投等債及新興債全數上漲。黃金則是
受惠避險資金低接買盤而從低點大幅回升。
► 歐元表現相對具有支撐，歐元區10月消費者
物價指數年增率攀升至4.0%，為三年來最
高，主要受能源成本所推動，市場預期歐洲
央行或將於12月再度升息支撐歐元，日圓方
面，日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，
未如市場預期偏鷹，令日圓持續弱勢盤整。

熱門議題
► 黃金價格歷經第二季修整，近日呈現大幅反
彈走勢，主要係因美國期中選舉後，市場憂
心AI產業監管將趨嚴，加上晶片與科技股遭
遇大幅修正，促使部分資金轉往黃金等防禦
型資產尋求避險。
► 根據世界黃金協會最新報告，第3季全球央行
淨買進321公噸黃金，較第二季明顯回升，年
增率達42%，創下自2024年第四季以來的單
季新高，前3季累計買進金額更是高達675公
噸，顯示黃金儲備已經列入全球央行必需資
產配置而且比重維持穩定增長態勢。
► 長期以來黃金具有抗通膨特性，觀察黃金與
聯邦基金利率走勢，從2020年之後，即便全
球歷經2次升息循環，黃金價格仍呈現穩健上
揚走勢，反應其抗通膨保值特性。此外，美
國財政赤字壓力難消，恐令美元長期購買力
被稀釋， 加 速 全 球 央 行 去 美 元 化 ， 有 鑑 於
此，建議黃金納入資產配置。
黃金走勢vs美元指數vs聯邦基金利率全球央行最新黃金儲備量
分裂政府中東僵局，資金輪動黃金避險

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，凱基證券整理；註：右上產業回報相關性為各產業過去3年之每週報酬相關性
► 醫療類股於美國第三季財報展現強勁的獲利
韌性，實際獲利優於預期程度為主要產業中
表現最佳，背後主要受惠大型製藥企業(如：
禮來、再生元製藥、吉利德科學)在減重藥、
癌症治療及創新藥領域之需求持續增長，帶
動整體產業獲利表現優於市場預期。
► 期中選舉後醫療政策輪廓逐漸明朗。民主黨
主張支持擴大醫療保險覆蓋、延續平價醫療
法案補貼以及緩和醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)
削減，有助提升醫療服務使用率；雖藥價談
判政策恐帶來部分定價壓力，但整體政策主
張仍對創新藥、生技研發及醫療保險產業具
潛在政策支持效果。
► 綜上，在AI投資回報率面臨市場檢驗之際，
市場資金持續尋求防禦性配置。醫療保健類
股與資訊科技類股相關性偏低，有助分散投
資組合對單一AI題材依賴，加上第三季獲利
動能轉強、創新藥需求穩健及產業能見度提
升，後續有望持續受惠資金輪動效應。

財報與政策題材雙利多，醫療類股成市場新寵兒

政策議題 民主黨傾向 共和黨傾向 兩黨共識對製藥產業潛在影響
降低藥價 依《通膨削減法案》框架，擴大醫療 補助計劃(Medicaid)藥價談判適用範圍 傾向透過最惠國定價機制來降低藥 價 兩黨均主張降低藥價 製藥企業面臨定價壓力
美國本土製造 透過研發補助、稅收優惠及產業政策 鼓勵藥廠回流美國生產 透過關稅、供應鏈重組及企業承諾 推動製藥產能回流 兩黨均支持醫藥供應鏈自主化 有利本土藥廠及原料藥供應鏈投資
醫療保險覆蓋
支持延長《平價醫療法案》補貼、擴
大醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)覆蓋範圍，
並緩和該補助計劃的削減
傾向由州政府及市場機制主導醫療
體系，限制聯邦政府支出
醫療的可負擔性為兩黨政策共同焦點，有助
增加就醫人口及處方藥使用量，支撐製藥、
醫療服務及保險業長期需求
FDA審查資源 支持增加FDA審查預算與人力配置 著重監管效率優化，額外資源投入 相對有限 兩黨均支持提升新藥審查效率 利多創新藥與生技公司

投資焦點必需消費
► 短期而言，檢視各類股在最近股災表現，美
國成長股、資訊科技、與通訊服務等高波動
度類股，表現明顯落後大盤，其中，資訊科
技與通訊服務波動度最大、跌幅最重，主要
係因美股科技巨頭財報不如預期、負自由現
金流量，疊加期中選舉後民主黨恐緊縮AI基
礎建設進程及資本支出見頂等不確定因素，
令市場 擔憂 導致 資 金流出 ；並轉 進核 心消
費、健康護理、防禦股、公用事業等防禦性
產業，報酬相對較穩健，受市場資金青睞。
► 長期而言，從2008迄今，核心消費類股長期
表現穩健上漲。與主要指數相比，面對近月
AI科技股修正，同期間費半大跌18%、那斯
達克跌幅6%、標普跌幅1%，而核心消費類
股大漲13%，反應其低波動、抗震韌性。
► 整體而言，從2015年至今，歷經2次通膨與
升息循環，核心消費受影響性低，有鑑於資
產配置角度，建議短期跟隨資金流向配置核
心消費股，提高投組抗震韌性。
必需消費長期表現穩健、抗通膨、低波動避險資金流入，必需消費類股單月表現最佳

配置必需消費類股，提高投組抗震韌性

股市
 隨著美國期中選舉結束，美伊衝突可望降溫，市場關注焦點將回歸基本面，惟AI資料中心建設進程
或將受到州政府緊縮政策而造成評價面壓力增添股價波動。短期可布局防禦股、分散配置於不同國
家與產業，增強抗震力、降低市場波動影響，長期而言，AI從全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高
的品質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨勢不變。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高的品
質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨
勢不變，非AI部分可關注必需消費產業、
醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 隨資金湧入，公債殖利率回落，惟根據最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升
息空間，立場偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升
時布局進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引

力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 聯準會12月份會議升息態度偏鷹，美元高檔整理。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理，日圓弱勢盤整
商品
 美國期中選舉結束，預期中東地緣衝突降溫，油價從近期高點回落，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金低點反彈，中長期維
持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期跟隨資金流向避險，中期擁抱AI政治紅利

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 1

据媒体报道， Meta 计划将 Muse Charm 定价为 300 美元，附赠 12 个⽉免费 5G 服务，并提供

Document 381: Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Wage Price Index_ award wage boost (2026-11-21).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-21
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#wages #inflation #labour
Q3 Wage Price Index: award wage boost
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
16:06 November 18 2026 Share
The minimum wage and award decision lifted wages growth, but unevenly – enterprise
arrangements responded more than individual arrangements, consistent with a softer
labour market. Q3 2026: 1.1%qtr, 3.5%yr.
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 1.1% in Q3 2026, slightly above Westpac’s forecast and the
market consensus for a 1.0% gain. That increased the annual growth rate to 3.5%yr – stronger than

the past six months, but a similar pace to 2025.
Today’s data also showcased the impact of the 2026 Fair Work Commission’s Annual Wage Review,
which handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award wages. This lifted wages in
aggregate, but the gains were uneven.
A significant share of the increase was driven by health care and social assistance, reflecting pay
rises for state government workers covered by enterprise agreements.
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3. Awards and enterprise arrangements accounted for 64%

of the lift, while individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share for Q3 since 2019.
Enterprise arrangements, which are often directly linked to award outcomes, were more responsive,
while individual arrangements were less so compared to previous September quarters. That is
consistent with a softer labour market and recent business survey evidence that suggests the rise in
private sector labour costs following the award decision quickly normalised.

Overall, we see the Q3 result as a step up in the level of wages from the award decision rather than
evidence of building underlying wage pressure but we will be watching whether the strength in
enterprise bargaining persists.

### Page 2

Wages by Bargaining Stream
Westpac has long believed focusing on wage inflation by wage bargaining stream can help to
understand how wages respond to the economic cycle. Australia’s labour market is split into three
categories of pay setting methods:
Australian wage adjustments, including the Fair Work Commission’s (FWC) minimum wage and award
rate decisions, tend to occur at the start of the new financial year in July. The FWC handed down a
4.75% increase for 2026, larger than the decisions from 2025 (3.50%) and 2024 (3.75%), but lower

than in 2023 (5.75%).
In original terms, the WPI lifted 1.7% in Q3 2026. Of that increase, 0.50ppts were attributed to awards,
which was broadly in line with our expectations. However, both individual and enterprise arrangements
surprised us to the upside, contributing 0.64ppts and 0.65ppts to the quarterly increase in wages
respectively.
National minimum wage and award rates, regulated by the Fair Work Ombudsman,
Enterprise agreements, or
Individual arrangements.

### Page 3

Compared to the same quarter in previous years, each stream's contribution was the strongest in
absolute terms since 2023, largely because the overall lift was bigger than in 2024 and 2025. After
accounting for the size of the increase, enterprise arrangements were responsible for 64% of the lift,
whereas individual arrangements contributed just 36%, the smallest share since 2019.
While the larger award wage decision pushed up the overall increase, it did not flow through evenly.

Many enterprise agreements are directly linked to award outcomes, either through explicit reference to
award rates or because the award decision sets a benchmark in bargaining, which helps explain why
this stream was more responsive. By contrast, wages set under individual arrangements can be more
discretionary and depend more on employers' circumstances, so they were less responsive than in
previous years. That may reflect a softer labour market, where weaker demand for workers reduces the

pressure on employers to lift pay.
Wages by Sector
By sector, private wages (excl. bonuses) rose 1.0% in Q3, pulling up the annual pace from 3.1%yr to
3.4%yr. This marks the strongest pace of wages growth in the private sector since 2023, at the time of
the Fair Work Commission’s decision to raise award wages by a much larger 5.75%.
Private sector pay is set through a mix of arrangements, including a significant share of individual
arrangements (as discussed above). Today's result suggests this year's award wage decision may

### Page 4

have had some flow-on effect to the broader private sector, though, as noted, the pass-through was
uneven and appears to have been stronger for enterprise arrangements than for individual
arrangements.
There was also an element of both breadth and scale when it comes to private sector wage gains. Half
of all private sector jobs recorded a wage increase in Q3, the largest share since this series began in
2016; and of those jobs that recorded an increase, average hourly wages rose 4.5%, the strongest

since Q3 2023 (5.8%).
Public wages continue to outpace the private sector, this time with a 1.3% lift in Q3, bringing the annual
growth pace to 3.9%yr, the strongest since Q4 2025. As expected, the bulk of the gain was driven by
pay rises under enterprise agreements at the state government level, particularly for front-line workers.
Additionally, given scheduled rises under existing APS-wide enterprise agreements for Commonwealth
employees had already been delivered in previous quarters, the Commonwealth level contribution to

public sector wages growth during Q3 was modest.
Relative to the private sector, the driver of public sector wage gains looks to be more about scale than
breadth – across the 38% of public sector jobs recording a wage increase during the quarter the
average hourly wage change was 4.0%, slightly under the record high of a 4.3% recorded back in Q4
2023.

### Page 5

Summary
The Q3 result shows that the larger minimum wage and award rate decision boosted wages but the
detail suggests the lift was driven more by workers with stronger bargaining power. Awards and
enterprise arrangements, where pay outcomes are more directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s
decision or to collective bargaining, whereas individual arrangements, which are more exposed to
labour market conditions, contributed the smallest share since 2019.

That is consistent with a softer labour market – the unemployment rate has risen ½ppt since the start of
the year, and we are expecting it to peak at 5.2% mid next year. The NAB business survey points the
same way, with the increase in labour costs reported by private sector firms following the award
decision quickly normalising. This suggests the Q3 result is better read as a temporary step up in the
level of wages from the award wage decision than as evidence of building underlying wage pressure,

though we will be watching whether the strength in enterprise bargaining persists.

Document 410: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor

Higher bond yields: how concerning?

Mark Zandi is Chief Economist of Moody’s Analytics. Below, he argues that the US economy 
is vulnerable rather than resilient and worries that high interest rates could deepen existing 
pockets of weakness and ultimately cause something to break in the financial system.   
The views stated herein are those of the interviewee and do not necessarily reflect those of Goldman Sachs.
Allison Nathan: Prior to the 
September 2026 Fed rate hike, you 
argued that a hike would do little to 
address supply-driven inflation but 
could damage already weak parts of 
the US economy. Where are you 
seeing the most softness? 
Mark Zandi: The clearest softness is 
in the labor market. Monthly payroll 
growth has averaged around 40,000 over the past year and has 
been essentially zero when excluding the healthcare sector. 
Hiring is moribund, hours worked are very low, and while the 
unemployment rate remains low, that is partly a reflection of 
declining labor force participation. Adjusting for that cyclical 
drop would put underlying unemployment in the mid-4% 
range—above the level consistent with full employment—and I 
expect the headline rate to move toward that level over the 
next 6-18 months. Real wage growth is, at best, flat, and for 
many Americans is actually declining, which is eroding 
purchasing power, particularly for lower- and middle-income 
households, who are increasingly drawing down savings to 
sustain spending. So, while the economy is still growing, the 
underlying labor market and household fundamentals point to 
very tenuous growth. 
 While the economy is still growing, the 
underlying labor market and household 
fundamentals point to very tenuous growth.”   
Allison Nathan: What is causing this softness? 
Mark Zandi: Policy choices are at the root of the weakness. 
Tariffs, while more consequential last year, continue to weigh 
on growth and boost inflation. Heavy-handed immigration policy 
is also an underappreciated source of weakness. Many firms 
losing immigrant workers cannot afford to raise wages enough 
to attract native-born workers, so they are raising prices to align 
demand with the supply they can produce with a reduced labor 
force. But the most immediate and significant driver of the 
softness is the Iran war, which has pushed up energy prices, 
further fueling inflation and weighing on growth.  
Allison Nathan: But US real GDP growth remains near 2%—
roughly the economy’s potential rate. Isn’t that a relatively 
healthy growth pace? 
Mark Zandi: Real GDP growth is indeed running at 2% yoy, but 
I estimate that AI-related activity accounts for roughly one-third 
of that, or around 0.6–0.7pp. Roughly half of that comes from AI 
capital spending, net of electronics imports for data centers, 
while the balance reflects wealth effects from the surge in AI-
related stock prices that have contributed to an almost doubling 
in the S&P 500 since ChatGPT debuted four years ago, boosting 
net worth and consumer spending, especially among high-
income households. So, the non-AI economy is growing at only 
around 1%–1.5%. That’s a pretty punk economy, and a difficult 
place to be. 
Allison Nathan: The September SEP indicated that most 
FOMC members expect one more rate hike this year, and 
the market is expecting the same. How damaging could 
further tightening be? 
Mark Zandi: While I expect another hike this year, with the 
economy already barely growing at potential, additional hikes 
could push growth below potential. That would further strain 
the already weak non-AI economy and trigger layoffs, 
significantly raising the likelihood of a self-reinforcing negative 
spiral. So, further tightening would be a risky endeavor. 
 With the economy already barely 
growing at potential, additional hikes could 
push growth below potential. That would 
further strain the already weak non-AI 
economy and trigger layoffs, significantly 
raising the likelihood of a self-reinforcing 
negative spiral.”   
Higher rates are already inflicting damage on the economy’s 
most rate-sensitive sectors, particularly housing and 
commercial real estate. Refinancing is in a deep freeze. Existing 
home sales have fallen to levels last seen during the teeth of 
the housing crash and the Global Financial Crisis. Home prices 
are moving sideways on average and outright declining in half 
the country. That could easily create broader problems for 
households, especially for those that bought homes in the last 
years after rates began to rise and have virtually no home 
equity. And while commercial real estate had begun to show 
signs of life, renewed rate increases are once again freezing 
activity and putting pressure on property values.  
Higher rates are also squeezing existing debtors as borrowing 
costs on credit cards, home loans, auto loans, and corporate 
debt rise. I am particularly worried about highly leveraged, often 
private equity-backed companies that have managed the higher 
rate environment by extending maturities and limiting near-term 
interest payments. That strategy cannot continue indefinitely if 
borrowing costs remain elevated or continue to rise.  
The equity market is another key area of concern. AI continues 
to propel the equity market, but it is not impervious to higher 
rates. Persistently elevated rates will eventually weigh on 
valuations, increasing the risk of a major correction that could 
quickly reverberate through the real economy as positive wealth 
effects reverse and consumer spending weakens. AI capex is 
also vulnerable as a higher cost of capital makes investment 
Interview with Mark Zandi

### Page 5

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 5 
Top of Mind Issue 152

more expensive and falling valuations make it harder to raise 
the capital necessary to sustain the current pace of the AI 
buildout. For now, AI is operating under its own momentum, 
supported by considerable optimism and euphoria. But if rates 
remain at these levels or higher for longer than a quarter or two, 
AI could become the part of the economy that breaks. 
Last but not least, higher rates will worsen the US fiscal 
situation, which is itself contributing to elevated yields today. 
While we have navigated this fiscal risk for some time, the 
fiscal situation is as bad as I have ever seen it. The deficit 
exceeds 6% of GDP, the primary deficit is above 3%, and debt 
is roughly 100% of GDP and on track to reach 125% in a 
decade. Annual federal interest payments are already well 
above $1tn and will rise rapidly if rates remain elevated. All told, 
I see a roughly one-in-five chance of a fiscal cliff event over the 
coming year—unlikely, but large enough to warrant attention. 
So, higher rates will weigh on the economy through several 
channels. Historically, rate increases of this magnitude have 
almost invariably caused something to break in the financial 
system. We cannot know exactly where the fault line will 
emerge, but the risk of these pockets of weakness becoming a 
broader problem is significant. 
 I view the distribution of possible 
outcomes as very flat and heavily skewed 
toward darker tail scenarios.”  
Allison Nathan: How do these risks translate into your 
forecasts for economic growth? 
Mark Zandi: My baseline forecast is relatively sanguine based 
on the assumption that the Iran war begins to wind down, 
allowing oil prices to decline to around $80/bbl as we move into 
next year. That should ease inflation, reduce Fed tightening 
concerns, and bring the 10-year Treasury yield back toward 
4.5% by early next year—a level the economy can digest.  
Importantly, though, I view the distribution of possible 
outcomes as very flat and heavily skewed toward darker tail 
scenarios. The war may not fade into the background, keeping 
energy prices elevated and raising the risk of further inflation 
pressure. That could prompt additional Fed tightening, push 
yields even higher, and ultimately cause something to break in 
the financial system.  
Meanwhile, the Treasury market has grown more fragile as 
traditional, price-insensitive buyers—including the Fed, banks, 
and foreign institutional investors—have stepped back, 
increasing the market’s reliance on highly leveraged, price-
sensitive hedge funds that could rush for the exits under stress. 
A debt-limit confrontation under a likely divided US government 
next year could provide the catalyst, particularly if lawmakers 
underestimate the consequences of a breach. In that scenario, 
an even sharper bond market selloff than we are currently 
experiencing could occur. While none of this is my baseline—
and I remain inherently optimistic because avoiding the darkest 
scenarios is largely a policy choice—I put the odds of a severe 
bond market selloff at around one-in-four and of the economy 
going completely off the rails for any of the reasons we have 
discussed at around one-in-three. So, the downside risks are 
significant. 
 I put the odds of a severe bond market 
selloff at around one-in-four and of the 
economy going completely off the rails for 
any of the reasons we have discussed at 
around one-in-three. So, the downside risks 
are significant.”  
Allison Nathan: The Fed has considerable room to cut 
rates. Should that provide any comfort that the worst-case 
scenarios may be avoided? 
Mark Zandi: That remains an open question. If inflation fades 
and allows the Fed to take its foot off the brake, rate cuts 
should bring yields down across the curve—though not back to 
pre-war levels. But in a darker scenario involving a debt limit 
crisis and even higher yields, short-term rate cuts would have 
little effect on the long end. The Fed would need to turn to 
alternative policy tools, including quantitative easing and bond 
purchases. That would amount to financial repression—a 
strategy similar to the one employed by the BoJ for many 
years—which could prove counterproductive by reducing the 
pressure on lawmakers to address the underlying problems 
while fueling even more persistent inflation. 
Allison Nathan: Why do you think others are more 
sanguine about the risks that higher interest rates pose to 
the US economy? 
Mark Zandi: Some likely see greater underlying economic 
strength and expect AI to drive much stronger growth. Others 
may believe that the neutral rate is higher, perhaps for the 
same reasons, while some may be more concerned about 
persistently higher inflation.  
But the more fundamental difference is the broader lens 
through which I currently view the economy. Many continue to 
describe the US economy as resilient, which I find fascinating, 
bordering on frustrating. In my view, the right word is 
vulnerable. Again, growth is pedestrian, the labor market is soft, 
which was further underscored by the much weaker-than- 
expected September jobs report, real wage growth is 
decelerating, inflation remains elevated, and interest rates are 
rising. That shift in perspective completely changes how one 
evaluates the risks ahead.

### Page 6

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 6 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Tobias Adrian is Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former Financial Counsellor and 
Director of the Monetary and Capital Markets Department at the IMF. Below, he argues that 
the global economy has been resilient despite higher bond yields and could very well remain 
so. But he sees several areas of concern in the economy and markets.  
The views stated herein are those of the interviewee and do not necessarily reflect those of Goldman Sachs, the IMF, IMF 
Management, or the IMF Executive Board.
Jenny Grimberg: The IMF has 
cautioned that sharp increases in 
global bond yields could create 
strains across economies and 
markets. How would you assess the 
global economy’s response to the 
recent rise in yields?  
Tobias Adrian: The global economy 
has so far responded to the rise in 
yields much the same way as it has to the shocks of the past 
seven years: resiliently. If somebody had told you in late 2019 
that the global economy would face a pandemic, multiple wars, 
deep geopolitical fragmentation, and trade and energy supply 
shocks, you would have been hard-pressed not to expect a 
global recession. The pandemic did, of course, trigger a 
contraction, but it was exceptionally short-lived despite the 
severe supply and demand shocks. And the global monetary 
tightening cycle that began in 2021 and accelerated sharply in 
2022 in response to the pandemic-related inflation surge did not 
trigger a recession at all, which is unusual by historical 
standards. So, the global economy has proven deeply resilient.   
Jenny Grimberg: What has that resilience owed to? 
Tobias Adrian: Three factors explain this resilience: policy, the 
adaptability of the economy, and some luck related to 
technological innovation. Over the past seven years, fiscal and 
monetary policymakers acted aggressively to stabilize economic 
activity. Fiscal policy provided substantial support during the 
pandemic, while central banks cut interest rates, expanded 
asset purchases, and established emergency standing facilities. 
That fiscal support has of course come at a significant long-term 
cost as debt-to-GDP ratios in advanced economies have surged 
since 2019, and deficits in many countries remain large, 
contributing to the rise in long-term yields. But policy has 
undoubtedly been critical to the economy’s resilience to date.  
The economy itself has also become more adaptable. A 
dividend of the pandemic is that firms have developed a greater 
ability to rewire global value chains quickly in response to a 
range of disruptions, from shifts in energy and food prices to 
geopolitical developments, which has helped the global 
economy more effectively absorb shocks.  
And luck has played a role in that the more recent challenges 
have coincided with the emergence and rapid adoption of 
generative AI technology, which has helped fuel expectations of 
a broad productivity boom and created massive demand for 
investment that is stimulating aggregate demand. If large 
language models (LLMs) had not come to the fore in November 
2022, global growth in the more recent period certainly would 
not have been as strong.  
Jenny Grimberg: As bond yields continue to climb, are you 
concerned that this resilience could falter? 
Tobias Adrian: So far, the rise in bond yields has not triggered 
a substantial, broad-based tightening in financial conditions or, 
more importantly, disruptions to market functioning or forced 
deleveraging that could damage markets and the economy. But 
I see three main areas of concern: the AI and technology 
complex, sovereign bond markets, and emerging markets (EMs).  
Three vulnerabilities stand out in the AI and technology 
complex. First, the interconnectivity and circularity of 
investment among many AI firms raises the risk that one firm’s 
earnings hit could fairly quickly spread across the broader 
complex. Second, many AI firms have taken on significant 
leverage, and investor leverage has also grown through the use 
of leveraged ETFs and derivatives. Leverage upon leverage can 
be problematic when adverse shocks hit. Third, while relatively 
subdued valuations suggest that the market is not in a 1999 
state of indiscriminate price appreciation, with investors 
continuing to differentiate across firms and sectors, the 
strength of the complex is predicated on strong earnings and 
high revenue growth, and it is not clear how long that will last. 
While my baseline outlook is positive, greater competition from 
open-source models, for example, could eat into AI firms’ 
profitability and pressure the broader value chain.    
Sovereign debt markets are another key concern. As I 
mentioned, global public debt is approaching 100% of GDP, and 
debt levels in major advanced economies exceed 120% of 
GDP. Those are staggering figures from a historical perspective. 
The investor base for sovereign bonds has also increasingly 
shifted to more leveraged investors who are more sensitive to 
macro developments. A key indicator to watch is auction tails—
the difference between the yield at which a government sells 
bonds at auction and the yield the market expected beforehand. 
Looking at 30-year bond auctions across G4 sovereign bond 
markets, the tails have grown over time, suggesting increased 
market vulnerability to supply. 5-to-10-year forward term premia 
have risen to pre-Global Financial Crisis (GFC) levels. So, the 
quantitative easing period has now been fully priced out of the 
long end of the global yield curve. And I see no good reason for 
term premia to decline in the foreseeable future. If anything, 
both expected short rates and the term premium will probably 
remain somewhat elevated.  
Finally, while EM economies have shown tremendous 
resilience through the shocks of the past seven years owing to 
stronger policy frameworks, resilient FX reserve buffers, and 
the development of local-currency bond markets that have 
reduced currency mismatches, vulnerabilities remain. 
Particularly striking is the K-shaped pattern of capital flows. 
Capital has flowed into EM sovereign bonds, while equity 
Interview with Tobias Adrian

### Page 7

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 7 
Top of Mind Issue 152

capital has flowed out on net as domestic investors direct their 
savings into global—and, in particular, US—technology stocks. 
So, even though EM equities have rallied over the past two 
years, capital flows have moved in the other direction. That 
leaves these countries’ external positions more leveraged, with 
less equity and more debt on net.  
Jenny Grimberg: Higher yields played a key role in the 2023 
regional banking crisis. Are you worried about banks today? 
Tobias Adrian: Not particularly. Banks generally do well when 
interest rates are elevated, because financial intermediation is 
fundamentally about spreads. When rates rise, asset yields 
often reprice faster than deposit costs, supporting bank net 
revenues. That partly explained the banking system’s overall 
resilience in 2023, even as a few outlier banks ran into trouble. 
Significant capital and liquidity buffers broadly absorbed mark-
to-market losses on fixed-income securities, while higher 
spreads supported profitability.  
Jenny Grimberg: What about residential and commercial 
real estate, where stresses were already visible? 
Tobias Adrian: Real estate markets are faring differently across 
countries. London’s housing market, for example, has cooled 
meaningfully, while other major financial centers like New York 
and Hong Kong continue to see strong home price appreciation. 
Real estate valuations are sensitive to long-term yields, and the 
pace of price appreciation will probably slow if yields remain at 
elevated levels. But with economic activity remaining relatively 
strong, immediate severe distress in either commercial or 
residential real estate seems unlikely.   
Jenny Grimberg: When would you begin to worry that 
higher yields are creating a financial stability problem?  
Tobias Adrian: The speed and magnitude of the yield move 
matter enormously. So far, even though bond yields have risen 
sharply, market functioning has remained orderly and no forced 
deleveraging appears to be occurring, as we discussed. I would 
be more concerned if the move in yields accelerated or became 
nonlinear, which could set off a damaging deleveraging spiral in 
which investors are forced to reduce leverage, adding to selling 
pressures and intensifying market stress. The question then 
becomes: who will catch the falling knife?  
And leveraged investors may not be the only source of selling 
pressures. Before central banks launched massive asset 
purchase programs to stabilize markets in March 2020, open-
ended funds were the sellers, while some hedge funds actually 
played a stabilizing role. Sharp selling pressure can even come 
from plain-vanilla mutual funds or ETFs. That said, it is 
reassuring that the major banks are well capitalized today, 
because it would be particularly worrisome if stress in the non-
bank sector coincided with forced asset sales in the banking 
sector. We saw that dynamic during the GFC and the Long-
Term Capital Management crisis.  
Jenny Grimberg: What should investors watch for warning 
signs of systemic stress? 
Tobias Adrian: I would watch repo market and money market 
functioning, because the early warning signs of stress typically 
show up in short-term funding markets. I would also look at a 
range of market-based indicators, including cross-currency and 
domestic basis measures, swap spreads, and convenience 
yields. I would also watch for a broader tightening of financial 
conditions, which can indicate that pressure is spreading 
beyond a particular market. And I would keep a close eye on 
implied volatility as a market-based assessment of risk that 
feeds directly into investors’ risk-management models. When 
implied volatility rises sharply, investors may need to cut 
positions, which can reinforce the underlying market move.   
Jenny Grimberg: If vulnerabilities were to become more 
acute, how well prepared are policymakers to respond? 
Tobias Adrian: Central banks retain a powerful ability to inject 
liquidity through repo operations, outright asset purchases, and 
emergency standing facilities, and in recent years they have 
repeatedly demonstrated that they can act quickly and 
decisively when market functioning deteriorates. That is distinct 
from the broader stance of monetary policy. Ongoing 
inflationary pressures mean that monetary policy remains on a 
tightening bias in many countries. But central banks can hike 
rates and inject liquidity at the same time.  
Fiscal policymakers are more constrained. Governments may 
still be willing to respond as forcefully as they did during the 
pandemic, but after taking such aggressive fiscal steps, less 
room exists to go big on fiscal policy today. Markets may also 
react to such support more negatively than in 2020 given 
already-elevated debt levels and ongoing concerns about fiscal 
sustainability. If monetary and fiscal authorities act together, as 
they did in 2020, that would temporarily mitigate market stress, 
but it would not resolve the longer-term fiscal problem.  
Hopefully, the recent rise in bond yields will feed back into the 
politics of fiscal policy and prompt an adjustment. Bond market 
pressure has already triggered policy shifts in some countries. 
The question is how far yields must rise before adjustment 
occurs more broadly, and whether that adjustment can happen 
without uncomfortable volatility. But until it occurs, fiscal 
policymakers will remain relatively constrained.     
Jenny Grimberg: Where does all of that leave you on the 
outlook for the global economy and markets? 
Tobias Adrian: It is important to distinguish between the 
baseline and the downside. My baseline is fairly positive. The 
global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in 
the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be 
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they 
do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same 
time, I do worry about downside risks. Beyond the 
vulnerabilities we have discussed, cyber risk is an important 
concern. So far, cyber risks have not been macro-critical, but 
they certainly could become so. AI could strengthen cyber 
defenses, but it could also enhance offensive capabilities. And 
while sophisticated institutions may be resilient to cyberattacks, 
many firms may lack the capabilities and know-how to defend 
themselves effectively. So, plenty of room exists to worry 
about the downside, but the offset is policymaking, especially 
monetary policymaking, as we have discussed. All of that 
leaves me vigilant but cautiously optimistic.
